\displaystyle l_{\theta}=\frac{\theta 4\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}}{2-\theta\alpha^{2}(2\lambda_{P}+L)},\text{ and }k_{\theta}=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\rho^{2}+l_{\theta})^{2}+4l_{\theta}}-\frac{(\rho^{2}+l_{\theta})}{2},

from the proof of Lemma
B.6
which also gives
\bar{\rho}^{2}=\rho^{2}+k_{\theta}+l_{\theta}
. Notice that we have the following inequality for any
\theta\leq\theta_{\varphi}^{g}
,

l_{\theta}=\frac{\theta 4\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}}{2-\theta\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L)}\leq\frac{\theta_{\varphi}^{g}4\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}}{2-\theta_{\varphi}^{g}\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L)}=l_{\theta_{\varphi}^{g}},

where we also used the fact that
\lambda_{P}=\frac{\vartheta}{2\alpha}
.
Since
l_{\theta}>0
, we can also write

\bar{\rho}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}(\rho^{2}+l_{\theta})+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\rho^{2}+l_{\theta})^{2}+4l_{\theta}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\frac{1}{2}(\rho^{2}+l_{\theta_{\varphi}^{g}})+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\rho^{2}+l_{\theta_{\varphi}^{g}})^{2}+4l_{\theta_{\varphi}^{g}}}=\bar{\bar{\rho}}^{(\varphi)},

for all
\theta\in(0,\theta_{\varphi}^{g}]
.

Therefore, we can convert the inequality (
107
) into

EV@R_{1-\zeta}[f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})]<\min_{0<\theta\leq\theta_{\varphi}^{g}}\frac{\sigma^{2}d\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L)}{(1-\bar{\bar{\rho}}^{(\varphi)})(2-\theta\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L))}+\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta}\log(1/\zeta)+(\bar{\bar{\rho}}^{(\varphi)})^{k}2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0}).

It can be seen that

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bar{h}(\theta)=\frac{\sigma^{2}d\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L)}{(1-\bar{\bar{\rho}}^{(\varphi)})(2-\theta\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L))}+\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta}\log(1/\zeta)+(\bar{\bar{\rho}}^{\varphi})^{k}2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0}),

is convex on
(0,\theta_{u}^{g})
for any
(\vartheta,\psi)
as given in Theorem
2
(recall that
\alpha
and
\bar{\bar{\rho}}^{(\varphi)}
also depend on
(\vartheta,\psi)
).
Let us introduce the notations

\displaystyle\mathtt{c}_{0}=\frac{d\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L)}{1-\bar{\bar{\rho}}^{(\varphi)}},\;\mathtt{c}_{1}=\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L),

so that the function
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bar{h}(\theta)
simplifies to

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bar{h}(\theta)=\frac{\sigma^{2}\mathtt{c}_{0}}{2-\theta\mathtt{c}_{1}}+\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta}\log(1/\zeta)+(\bar{\bar{\rho}}^{(\varphi)})^{k}2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0}),

where its derivative,
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bar{h}^{\prime}(\theta)
, is given as

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bar{h}^{\prime}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma^{2}\mathtt{c}_{0}\mathtt{c}_{1}}{(2-\theta\mathtt{c}_{1})^{2}}-\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta^{2}}\log(1/\zeta)=\frac{\sigma^{2}c_{0}c_{1}\theta^{2}-2\sigma^{2}(2-\theta\mathtt{c}_{1})^{2}\log(1/\zeta)}{\theta^{2}(2-\theta\mathtt{c}_{1})^{2}}.

(108)

Notice that we have

\theta_{u}^{g}=\frac{2(1-\rho^{2})}{8\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}+\alpha(1-\rho^{2})(\vartheta+\alpha L)}<\frac{2}{\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L)}=\frac{2}{\mathtt{c}_{1}},

by the definition of
\theta_{u}^{g}
. Therefore, we have
\theta<\frac{2}{\mathtt{c}_{1}}
under the condition (
37
). Hence, we can see that polynomial at the numerator in (
108
) has only one root
\theta_{*}
on
[0,\frac{2}{\mathtt{c}_{1}}]
which is given by

\theta_{*}=\frac{2}{\mathtt{c}_{1}+\sqrt{\frac{\mathtt{c}_{0}\mathtt{c}_{1}}{2\log(1/\zeta)}}}<\frac{2}{\mathtt{c}_{1}}.

Moreover, we can also observe that

\displaystyle\mathtt{c}_{0}\mathtt{c}_{1}\theta^{2}-2(2-\theta\mathtt{c}_{1})^{2}\log(1/\zeta)\leq 0,

\displaystyle\text{ if }0\leq\theta\leq\theta_{*},

\displaystyle\mathtt{c}_{0}\mathtt{c}_{1}\theta^{2}-2(2-\theta\mathtt{c}_{1})^{2}\log(1/\zeta)>0,

\displaystyle\text{ if }\theta_{*}<\theta\leq\frac{2}{\mathtt{c}_{1}}.

This shows us that the function
h
is decreasing on
(0,\theta_{*}]

and increasing on
(\theta_{*},\frac{2}{\mathtt{c}_{1}})
; therefore, it is locally convex on the region
(0,\frac{2}{\mathtt{c}_{1}})
. So far, we obtain the following inequality

EV@R_{1-\zeta}[f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})]{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq}\min_{0<\theta\leq\theta^{g}_{\varphi}}\underbrace{\frac{\sigma^{2}d\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L)}{(1-\bar{\bar{\rho}}^{(\varphi)})(2-\theta\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L))}+\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta}\log(1/\zeta)+(\bar{\bar{\rho}}^{(\varphi)})^{k}2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bar{h}(\theta)},

where
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bar{h}(\theta)
is convex on
(0,\theta_{\varphi}^{g}]
and
\varphi
is a constant between
0
and
1
. Therefore, the minimization problem on the right-hand side of the inequality above can be solved:

EV@R_{1-\zeta}[f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})]{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq}\begin{cases}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bar{h}(\theta_{*}),&\text{ if }\theta_{*}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\theta_{\varphi}^{g},\\
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bar{h}(\theta_{\varphi}^{g}),&\text{ otherwise }.\end{cases}

(109)

Notice that for any fixed constant
\varphi\in(0,1)
, the inequality (
41
) implies

\displaystyle\log(1/\zeta)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{d}{2(1-\bar{\bar{\rho}}^{(\varphi)})}\left(\frac{\theta_{\varphi}^{g}\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L)}{2-\theta_{\varphi}^{g}\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L)}\right)^{2}=\frac{\mathtt{c}_{0}\mathtt{c}_{1}}{2}\left(\frac{\theta_{\varphi}^{g}}{2-\theta_{\varphi}^{g}\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L)}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\implies\frac{2-\theta_{\varphi}^{g}\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L)}{\theta_{\varphi}^{g}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\mathtt{c}_{0}\mathtt{c}_{1}}{2\log(1/\zeta)}}

\displaystyle\implies\frac{2}{\theta_{\varphi}^{g}}\leq c_{1}+\sqrt{\frac{c_{0}c_{1}}{2\log(1/\zeta)}}

Recall that
\mathtt{c}_{1}=\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L)
, hence above inequalities imply that under condition (
41
) we have

\theta_{*}=\frac{2}{\mathtt{c}_{1}+\sqrt{\frac{\mathtt{c}_{0}\mathtt{c}_{1}}{2\log(1/\zeta)}}}\leq\theta_{\varphi}^{g},

and we have

\displaystyle h(\theta_{*})

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma^{2}\mathtt{c}_{0}}{2-\theta_{*}\mathtt{c}_{1}}+\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta_{*}}\log(1/\zeta)+\big(\bar{\bar{\rho}}^{(\varphi)}\big)^{k}2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0}),

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{\mathtt{c}_{0}}}{2}\Big(\sqrt{2\mathtt{c}_{1}\log(1/\zeta)}+\sqrt{\mathtt{c}_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\sqrt{2\mathtt{c}_{1}\log(1/\zeta)}\Big(\sqrt{2\mathtt{c}_{1}\log(1/\zeta)}+\sqrt{\mathtt{c}_{0}}\Big)+\big(\bar{\bar{\rho}}^{(\varphi)}\big)^{k}2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0}),

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\Big(\sqrt{2\mathtt{c}_{1}\log(1/\zeta)}+\sqrt{\mathtt{c}_{0}}\Big)^{2}+(\bar{\bar{\rho}}^{(\varphi)})^{k}2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0}).

The computations above show that (
109
) is equivalent to (
42
) by the definitions of
\mathtt{c}_{1}
and
\mathtt{c}_{0}
if the condition (
41
) holds and, similarly, the inequality (
43
) directly follows from writing
h(\theta_{\varphi}^{g})
explicitly for the case
\theta_{\varphi}^{g}>\theta_{*}
in (
109
).
Lastly, we can directly derive the inequality (
44
) using our results so far. In particular, taking the limit superior of the inequality (
38
) gives us

\displaystyle\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim\sup}\;\mathbf{r}_{k,\sigma^{2}}(\theta)

\displaystyle\leq\lim\sup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\sigma^{2}d\alpha\big(\vartheta+\alpha L\big)}{(1-\bar{\rho}^{2})(2-\theta\alpha\big(\vartheta+\alpha L\big))}+\bar{\rho}^{2k}2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{r}_{\sigma^{2}}(\theta)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma^{2}d\alpha\big(\vartheta+\alpha L\big)}{(1-\bar{\rho}^{2})(2-\theta\alpha\big(\vartheta+\alpha L\big))}.

Therefore, the EV@R at
\zeta
-confidence level admits the bound

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim\sup}\;EV@R_{1-\zeta}[f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})]

\displaystyle=\inf_{0<\theta}\mathbf{r}_{\sigma^{2}}(\theta)+\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta}\log(1/\zeta),

\displaystyle\leq\min_{0<\theta\leq\theta_{\varphi}^{g}}\underbrace{\frac{\sigma^{2}d\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L)}{(1-\bar{\bar{\rho}}^{(\varphi)})(2-\theta\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L))}+\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta}\log(1/\zeta)}_{\tilde{h}(\theta)}.

Notice that
\tilde{h}(\theta)=h(\theta)-(\bar{\bar{\rho}}^{(\varphi)})^{k}2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})
and share the same local minimum, i.e.,
\tilde{h}^{\prime}(\theta_{*})=0
as well; and
\tilde{h}(\theta)
is also convex on
(0,\theta_{\varphi}^{g}]
. It follows from our discussion on
\theta_{*}
that
\tilde{h}(\theta_{*})=\min_{0<\theta\leq\theta_{\varphi}^{g}}\tilde{h}(\theta)
which gives the desired inequality (
44
).

B.4
The Proof of Corollary
2

We are going to show (
45
) using Chernoff bound for
f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})
and implementing the inequality (
38
) provided at Proposition
3
. Particularly, Chernoff bound gives us

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})\geq t_{\zeta}\}\leq e^{-\frac{\theta t_{\zeta}}{2\sigma^{2}}}\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}(f(x_{k})-f(x_{*}))}].

Since the function
\log(x)
is increasing on
x>0
, we can convert above inequality into,

\displaystyle\log(\mathbb{P}\{f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})\geq t_{\zeta}\})\leq\log\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}(f(x_{k})-f(x_{*}))}]-\frac{\theta t_{\zeta}}{2\sigma^{2}}=\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\mathbf{r}_{k,\sigma^{2}}(\theta)-\frac{\theta t_{\zeta}}{2\sigma^{2}}.

Moreover, we can see that the inequality (
38
) holds by the statement of Corollary
2
. This yields the bound,

\log(\mathbb{P}\{f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})\geq t_{\zeta}\})\leq\frac{\theta d\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L)}{2(1-\bar{\rho}^{2})(2-\theta\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L))}+\frac{\theta\bar{\rho}^{2k}\mathcal{V}_{P}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})}{\sigma^{2}}-\frac{\theta t_{\zeta}}{2\sigma^{2}},

where we drop
\vartheta,\psi
dependency on the notations
\alpha_{\vartheta,\psi}
and
\bar{\rho}_{\vartheta,\psi}^{2}
for simplicity. The result follows from taking the exponential power of both sides. The next inequality (
46
), on the other hand, follows from (
7
), (
42
), and (
43
). Notice that the inequalities (
42
) and (
43
) imply

EV@R_{1-\zeta}[f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})]\leq\bar{E}_{1-\zeta}(\vartheta,\psi)+(\bar{\bar{\rho}}^{(\varphi)}_{\vartheta,\psi})^{k}2\mathcal{V}_{P}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0}),

Let us denote
EV@R_{1-\zeta}[f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})]
by
EV@R_{1-\zeta}
with a slight abuse notation.Therefore, combining these inequalities with (
7
) yields

\displaystyle\zeta\geq\mathbb{P}\{f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})\geq EV@R_{1-\zeta}\}\geq\mathbb{P}\{f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})\geq\bar{E}_{1-\zeta}(\vartheta,\psi)+(\bar{\bar{\rho}}^{(\varphi)}_{\vartheta,\psi})^{k}2\mathcal{V}_{P}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})\},

and we conclude.

B.5
Proof of Proposition
4

Notice that the following inequality holds at each iterate
x_{k}
,

\displaystyle\|x_{k+1}-x_{*}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\|x_{k}-x_{*}-\alpha\tilde{\nabla}f(x_{k})\|^{2},

\displaystyle=\|x_{k}-x_{*}-\alpha\nabla f(x_{k})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad-2\alpha(x_{k}-x_{*}-\alpha\nabla f(x_{k}))^{\top}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}+\alpha^{2}\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|^{2}.

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}=\tilde{\nabla}f(y_{k})-\nabla f(y_{k})
. Let
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}m_{k}^{GD}=-2\alpha(x_{k}-x_{*}-\alpha\nabla f(x_{k}))
, then we can write,

\displaystyle\|x_{k+1}-x_{*}\|^{2}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho_{GD}(\alpha)^{2}\|x_{k}-x_{*}\|^{2}+(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}m_{k}^{GD})^{\top}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}+\alpha^{2}\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|^{2}.

(110)

where
\rho_{GD}(\alpha)=\max\{|1-\alpha\mu|,|1-\alpha L|\}
, see e.g.
[
30
]
. For simplicity, we adopt the notation
\rho_{GD}:=\rho_{GD}(\alpha)
in the rest of the proof. Next, we will show the inequality

\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta L}{4\sigma^{2}}\|x_{k+1}-x_{*}\|^{2}}]<\prod_{j=0}^{k}\left(1-\theta\frac{\alpha^{2}L}{2}\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2j}\right)^{-d/2}e^{\frac{\theta L}{4\sigma^{2}}\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2(k+1)}\|x_{0}-x_{*}\|^{2}},

(111)

where
\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2}=\frac{2\rho^{2}_{GD}}{2-\theta\alpha^{2}L}<1
by our assumption on
\theta
. Since the inequality (
110
) holds pointwise

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta L}{4\sigma^{2}}\|x_{k+1}-x_{*}\|^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta L}{4\sigma^{2}}\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho^{2}_{GD}\|x_{k}-x_{*}\|^{2}+(m_{k}^{GD})^{\top}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}+\alpha^{2}\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|^{2}\right)}\right],

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta L}{4\sigma^{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho^{2}_{GD}\|x_{k}-x_{*}\|^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta L}{4\sigma^{2}}\left((\color[rgb]{0,0,0}m_{k}^{GD})^{\top}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}+\alpha^{2}\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|^{2}\right)}~\vline~\mathcal{F}_{k}\right]\right].

Notice that
\theta<\frac{2(1-\rho^{2}_{GD})}{\alpha^{2}L}<\frac{2}{\alpha^{2}L}
and the noise
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}
is independent from
\mathcal{F}_{k}
by Assumption
1
; therefore, we can apply Lemma
B.4
to the conditional expectation
13
13

13

By setting
m\rightarrow m_{k}
,
w\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}
,
\lambda_{p}+L/2\rightarrow 1
and
\theta\rightarrow\frac{\theta L}{2}
in Lemma
B.4
.
,

\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta L}{4\sigma^{2}}\|x_{k+1}-x_{*}\|^{2}}\right]\leq\left(1-\theta\frac{\alpha^{2}L}{2}\right)^{-d/2}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta L}{4\sigma^{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho_{GD}^{2}\|x_{k}-x_{*}\|^{2}+\frac{\theta^{2}L^{2}\|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}m_{k}^{GD}\|^{2}}{16\sigma^{2}(2-\theta\alpha^{2}L)}}\right].

By
L
-smoothness and
\mu
-strong convexity we can write,

\|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}m_{k}^{GD}\|^{2}=4\alpha^{2}\|x_{k}-x_{*}-\alpha\nabla f(x_{k})\|^{2}\leq 4\alpha^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho_{GD}^{2}\|x_{k}-x_{*}\|^{2}.

(112)

Hence, we obtain

\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta L}{4\sigma^{2}}\|x_{k+1}-x_{*}\|^{2}}\right]\leq\left(1-\theta\frac{\alpha^{2}L}{2}\right)^{-d/2}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta L}{4\sigma^{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho_{GD}^{2}\left(1+\frac{\theta\alpha^{2}L}{2-\theta\alpha^{2}L}\right)\|x_{k}-x_{*}\|^{2}}\right].

On the other hand, we can write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta L}{4\sigma^{2}}\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2}\|x_{k}-x_{*}\|^{2}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta L\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2}}{4\sigma^{2}}\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho_{GD}^{2}\|x_{k-1}-x_{*}\|^{2}+(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}m_{k-1}^{GD})^{\top}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}+\alpha^{2}\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}\|^{2}\right)}\right],

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}=\tilde{\nabla}f(y_{k-1})-\nabla f(y_{k-1})
. The fact that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}
is independent from
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
and
\theta\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2}<\theta<\frac{2}{\alpha^{2}L}
imply that we can again incorporate Lemma
B.4
using tower property of conditional expectations and obtain,
14
14

14

By setting
m\rightarrow m_{k}
,
w\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}
,
\theta\rightarrow\frac{\theta L\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2}}{2}
and
\lambda_{P}+L/2\rightarrow 1
in Lemma
B.4
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta L\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2}}{4\sigma^{2}}\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho_{GD}^{2}\|x_{k-1}-x_{*}\|^{2}+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(m_{k-1}^{GD})^{\top}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}+\alpha^{2}\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}\|^{2}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta L\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2}}{4\sigma^{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho_{GD}^{2}\|x_{k-1}-x_{*}\|^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta L\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2}}{4\sigma^{2}}\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(m_{k-1}^{GD})^{\top}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}+\alpha^{2}\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}\|^{2}\right)}~\vline~\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\right],

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\left(1-\theta\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2}\frac{\alpha^{2}L}{2}\right)^{-d/2}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta L\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2}}{4\sigma^{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho^{2}_{GD}\|x_{k-1}-x_{*}\|^{2}+\frac{\theta^{2}\bar{\rho}^{4}_{GD}L^{2}\|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}m_{k-1}^{GD}\|^{2}}{16\sigma^{2}(2-\theta\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2}\alpha^{2}L)}}\right].

Since
\bar{\rho}_{GD}<1
,

\frac{\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{4}}{2-\theta\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2}\alpha^{2}L}<\frac{1}{2-\theta\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2}\alpha^{2}L}<\frac{1}{2-\theta\alpha^{2}L}.

Using the inequality (
112
), we can see that
\|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}m_{k-1}^{GD}\|^{2}\leq 4\alpha^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho^{2}_{GD}\|x_{k-1}-x_{*}\|^{2}
. Similarly, This implies,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta L\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2}}{4\sigma^{2}}\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho^{2}_{GD}\|x_{k-1}-x_{*}\|^{2}+(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}m_{k-1}^{GD})^{\top}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}+\alpha^{2}\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}\|^{2}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\theta\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2}\frac{\alpha^{2}L}{2}\right)^{-d/2}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta L\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2}}{4\sigma^{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho^{2}_{GD}\left(1+\frac{\theta\alpha^{2}L}{2-\theta\alpha^{2}L}\right)\|x_{k-1}-x_{*}\|^{2}}\right],

\displaystyle=\left(1-\theta\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2}\frac{\alpha^{2}L}{2}\right)^{-d/2}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta L}{4\sigma^{2}}\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{4}\|x_{k-1}-x_{*}\|^{2}}\right].

Combining everything, we get

\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta L}{4\sigma^{2}}\|x_{k+1}-x_{*}\|^{2}}]<\left(1-\theta\frac{\alpha^{2}L}{2}\right)^{-d/2}\left(1-\theta\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2}\frac{\alpha^{2}L}{2}\right)^{-d/2}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta L}{4\sigma^{2}}\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{4}\|x_{k-1}-x_{*}\|^{2}}\right].

Repeating these arguments for each
x_{i}
for
i=1,..,k-1
, we can show the desired inequality (
111
):

\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta L}{4\sigma^{2}}\|x_{k+1}-x_{*}\|^{2}}]<\prod_{j=0}^{k}\left(1-\theta\big(\frac{\alpha^{2}L}{2}\big)\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2j}\right)^{-d/2}e^{\frac{\theta L}{4\sigma^{2}}\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2(k+1)}\|x_{0}-x_{*}\|^{2}}.

Recall that the function
f
is
L
-smooth; therefore, it satisfies
f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})\leq\frac{L}{2}\|x_{k}-x_{*}\|^{2}
and this directly gives us the bound on finite-horizon entropic risk measure:

\displaystyle\mathbf{r}_{k,\sigma^{2}}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta}\log\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}(f(x_{k})-f(x_{*}))}]\leq\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta}\log\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta L}{4\sigma^{2}}\|x_{k}-x_{*}\|^{2}}],

\displaystyle<-\frac{\sigma^{2}d}{\theta}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\log\left(1-\theta(\frac{\alpha^{2}L}{2})\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2j}\right)+\frac{L\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2k}}{2}\|x_{0}-x_{*}\|^{2}.

Using the property
\log(x)\geq 1-\frac{1}{x}
for
x\in(0,1]
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{r}_{k,\sigma^{2}}(\theta)

\displaystyle<\sigma^{2}d\alpha^{2}L\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2j}}{2-\theta\alpha^{2}L\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2j}}+\frac{L\bar{\rho}^{2k}_{GD}}{2}\|x_{0}-x_{*}\|^{2},

\displaystyle<\frac{\sigma^{2}d\alpha^{2}L}{(2-\theta\alpha^{2}L)(1-\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2})}+\frac{L\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2k}}{2}\|x_{0}-x_{*}\|^{2},

where the last inequality follows from
2-\theta\alpha^{2}L<2-\theta\alpha^{2}L\bar{\rho}^{2j}_{GD}
for
\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{2}<1
. Writing
\bar{\rho}^{2}_{GD}=\frac{2\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho^{2}_{GD}}{2-\theta\alpha^{2}L}
back into the bound above gives the inequality (
48
). Lastly, taking the limit superior on the right-hand side of the inequality (
48
) yields (
49
)

B.6
Proof of Theorem
4

Most of the proof is similar to the proof of Theorem
3
and relies on the bounds provided in Proposition
4
. Firstly notice that

\displaystyle EV@R_{1-\zeta}[f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})]

\displaystyle=\inf_{0<\theta}\mathbf{r}_{k,\sigma^{2}}(\theta)+\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta}\log(1/\zeta),

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{0<\theta<\frac{2(1-\rho^{2})}{\alpha^{2}L}}\frac{\sigma^{2}d\alpha^{2}L}{2(1-\rho^{2})-\theta\alpha^{2}L}+\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta}\log(1/\zeta)+\left(\frac{\rho^{2}}{1-\theta\alpha^{2}(L/2)}\right)^{k}\frac{L\|x_{0}-x_{*}\|^{2}}{2}.

We fix
\varphi\in[\frac{1}{1+\sqrt{\frac{d}{2\log(1/\zeta)}}},1)
and define
\theta_{\varphi}:=\frac{2\varphi(1-\rho^{2})}{\alpha^{2}L}
. Then, we can write

\frac{\rho^{2}}{1-\theta\alpha^{2}(L/2)}\leq\frac{\rho^{2}}{1-\theta_{\varphi}\alpha^{2}(L/2)},

for all
\theta\leq\theta_{\varphi}
. This implies

EV@R_{1-\zeta}[f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})]\\
\leq\min_{0<\theta\leq\theta_{\varphi}}\frac{\sigma^{2}d\alpha^{2}L}{2(1-\rho^{2})-\theta\alpha^{2}L}+\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta}\log(1/\zeta)+\left(\frac{\rho^{2}}{1-\theta_{\varphi}\alpha^{2}(L/2)}\right)^{k}\frac{L\|x_{0}-x_{*}\|^{2}}{2}.

(113)

Let us introduce
\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{(\varphi)}=\frac{\rho^{2}}{1-\theta_{\varphi}\alpha^{2}(L/2)}
for simplicity and denote the function that depends on
\theta
in the inequality above by
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bar{h}_{GD}(\theta)
. That is

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bar{h}_{GD}(\theta):=\frac{\sigma^{2}d\alpha^{2}L}{2(1-\rho^{2})-\theta\alpha^{2}L}+\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta}\log(1/\zeta)+\big(\bar{\rho}_{GD}^{(\varphi)}\big)^{k}\frac{L\|x_{0}-x_{*}\|^{2}}{2}.

Its derivative
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bar{h}_{GD}^{\prime}(\theta)
is given as

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bar{h}_{GD}^{\prime}(\theta)=\frac{\sigma^{2}d(\alpha^{2}L)^{2}}{(2(1-\rho^{2})-\theta\alpha^{2}L)^{2}}-\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta^{2}}\log(1/\zeta),

whose root
\theta_{*}
is given as:

\theta_{*}=\frac{2(1-\rho^{2})}{\alpha^{2}L(1+\sqrt{\frac{d}{2\log(1/\zeta)}})}\leq\theta_{\varphi},

where the last inequality follows from the choice of
\varphi
and
\theta_{*}=\theta_{\varphi}
if
\varphi=\frac{1}{1+\sqrt{\frac{d}{2\log(1/\zeta)}}}=\varphi_{GD}
. On the other hand, we can see that
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bar{h}_{GD}^{\prime}(\theta)<0
on
(0,\theta_{*})
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bar{h}_{GD}^{\prime}(\theta)\geq 0
on
[\theta_{*},\theta_{\varphi}]
, which shows that the solution of the minimization problem on the right-hand side of the inequality (
113
) is
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bar{h}_{GD}(\theta_{*})
. The desired result follows directly from setting
\varphi=\varphi_{GD}
using
EV@R_{1-\zeta}[f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})]\leq\bar{h}_{GD}(\theta_{*})
.

Appendix C
Proofs of Section
6

We start with a helper lemma about the properties of sub-Gaussian distributions before proceeding to the proof of Proposition
5
. The following results in Lemma
C.1
are well-known (see
[
60
]
). We provide them for the sake of completeness.

Lemma C.1
.

The following inequalities hold,

1.

Under Assumption
2
, we have the moment bounds

\mathbb{E}[\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|^{n}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~|~\mathcal{F}_{k}}]\leq(2\sigma^{2})^{n/2}n\Gamma(\frac{n}{2}),

(114)

for any
n\geq 1
, where
\Gamma
is the Gamma function.

2.

The moment generating function of
\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|
admits the following bound

\mathbb{E}[e^{\theta\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~|~\mathcal{F}_{k}}]\leq e^{4\sigma^{2}\theta^{2}},\;\;\;\text{ \color[rgb]{0,0,0} for any }\theta\geq 0.

(115)

3.

Suppose
0\leq\theta\leq\frac{1}{8\sigma^{2}}
, then the moment generating function of
\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|^{2}
has the following property

\mathbb{E}[e^{\theta\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|^{2}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~|~\mathcal{F}_{k}}]\leq e^{8\sigma^{2}\theta},

(116)

Proof.

We are first going to prove (1). Notice that integral representation of expectation together with Condition (
55
) yield

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|^{n}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~|~\mathcal{F}_{k}}]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\{\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|^{n}\geq t{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~|~\mathcal{F}_{k}}\}dt,

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\{\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|\geq t^{1/n}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~|~\mathcal{F}_{k}}\}dt\leq 2\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{t^{2/n}}{2\sigma^{2}}}dt.

Applying change of variable
u=\frac{t^{2/n}}{2\sigma^{2}}
yields

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{E}[\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|^{n}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~|~\mathcal{F}_{k}}]\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}n(2\sigma^{2})^{n/2}e^{-u}u^{n/2-1}du=(2\sigma^{2})^{n/2}n\Gamma(\frac{n}{2}).}

In order to show (2) we are going to use (
114
) and Taylor expansion of exponential function. The dominated convergence theorem implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[e^{\theta\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~|~\mathcal{F}_{k}}]

\displaystyle=1+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\theta^{n}}{n!}\mathbb{E}[\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|^{n}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~|~\mathcal{F}_{k}}]\leq 1+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\theta^{n}(2\sigma^{2})^{n/2}n\Gamma(\frac{n}{2})}{n!},

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(2\theta^{2}\sigma^{2})^{n}2n\Gamma(n)}{(2n)!}+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(2\theta^{2}\sigma^{2})^{n+1/2}(2n+1)\Gamma(n+1/2)}{(2n+1)!},

\displaystyle\leq 1+2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(2\sigma^{2}\theta^{2})^{n}n!}{(2n)!}+\sqrt{2\sigma^{2}\theta^{2}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(2\sigma^{2}\theta^{2})^{n}\Gamma(n+1/2)}{(2n)!}

Recall that
2(k!)^{2}\leq(2k)!
and duplication formula,
\Gamma(k+1/2)=\frac{(2k)!}{4^{k}k!}\sqrt{\pi}
(see
[
61
, 6.1.18]
). Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[e^{\theta\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~|~\mathcal{F}_{k}}]

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(2\sigma^{2}\theta^{2})^{n}}{n!}+\sqrt{2\sigma^{2}\theta^{2}\pi}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}(\frac{\sigma^{2}\theta^{2}}{2})^{n},

\displaystyle=e^{2\sigma^{2}\theta^{2}}+\sqrt{2\sigma^{2}\theta^{2}\pi}(e^{\sigma^{2}\theta^{2}/2}-1).

Notice that
\sqrt{x}\leq e^{\frac{x}{4}}
for all
x>0
; therefore,
\sqrt{2\sigma^{2}\theta^{2}\pi}(e^{\sigma^{2}\theta^{2}/2}-1)\leq e^{2\sigma^{2}\theta^{2}}(e^{\sigma^{2}\theta^{2}/2}-1)
which yields (
115
). Lastly, we show (3):

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[e^{\theta\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}}\|^{2}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~|~\mathcal{F}_{k}}]=1+\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}n}=1}^{\infty}\frac{\theta^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}n}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}n}!}\mathbb{E}[\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}}\|^{2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}n}}]\leq 1+\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}n}=1}^{\infty}\frac{\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}n}}(2\sigma^{2})^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}n}}(2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}n})\Gamma({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}n})}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}n}!}=1+\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}n}=1}^{\infty}(4\sigma^{2}\theta)^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}n}.

Note that for
x\leq\frac{1}{2}
the inequality
\frac{1}{1-x}\leq e^{2x}
holds and the condition
\theta\leq\frac{1}{8\sigma^{2}}
implies
4\sigma^{2}\theta\leq\frac{1}{2}
which gives the desired result.
∎

C.1
Proof of Proposition
5

For simplicity of the presentation, as before, we drop the dependancy to the parameters
(\vartheta,\psi)
. Since the parameters are as given in Theorem
2
, they satisfy (
MI
). This implies that Lemma
B.3
holds for the Lyapunov function
\mathcal{V}_{P}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})
. Hence, we can write

\mathbb{E}[f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})]\leq\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})]\leq\rho^{2}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1})]+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\mathbb{E}[\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}\|^{2}],

where we used the fact that
\mathbb{E}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{F}_{k-1}]=0
. On the other hand, the inequality (
114
) yields

\mathbb{E}[\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}\|^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}\|^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}]]\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq 2\sigma^{2}.

Therefore, we obtain

\mathbb{E}[f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})]\leq\rho^{2}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-1)]+\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L)\sigma^{2}\leq\rho^{2k}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})+\sigma^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\frac{1-\rho^{2k}}{1-\rho^{2}}.

This completes the proof.

C.2
Proof of Proposition
6

Similar to before, we drop the
(\vartheta,\psi)
dependency in the notations for simplicity. Denote
\mathcal{V}_{P}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})
by
\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})
, and also denote the largest eigenvalue of
d
-by-
d
principle submatrix of the matrix
P
by
\lambda_{P}
which can be computed as
\lambda_{P}=\frac{\vartheta}{2\alpha}
under the premise of Theorem
2
. Similar to the proof of Proposition
3
, we are first going to provide a bound on moment generating function of
f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})
. More specifically, we will show that

\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}(f(x_{k})-f(x_{*}))}]\leq e^{2\theta\alpha^{2}\big(L+2\lambda_{P}\big)\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\hat{\rho}^{2j}+\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\hat{\rho}^{2k}2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})+2\theta\hat{\rho}^{2(k-1)}\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})},

(117)

For this purpose, we introduce the notation

\displaystyle\hat{k}_{\theta}=\frac{1}{2}\left[\sqrt{(\rho^{2}+32\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}\theta)^{2}+128\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}\theta}-(\rho^{2}+32\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}\theta)\right],

which satisfies the following relation

(\hat{k}_{\theta})^{2}+\hat{k}_{\theta}(\rho^{2}+32\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}\theta)=32\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}\theta\implies\hat{k}_{\theta}=\frac{32\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}\theta}{\rho^{2}+\hat{k}_{\theta}+32\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}\theta}.

(118)

The rest of the proof is similar to the proof of Lemma
B.6
where our aim will be to show the inequality

\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1})+\hat{k}_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})\big)}\right]<e^{2\theta\alpha^{2}\big(L+2\lambda_{P}\big)\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\hat{\rho}^{2j}}\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\hat{\rho}^{2k}\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{1})+\hat{k}_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})\big)}].

(119)

By Assumption
2
, the noise
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}=\tilde{\nabla}f(y_{k})-\nabla f(y_{k})
is independent from
\mathcal{F}_{k}
. Moreover, since we already adopted the parameters of Theorem
2
, they satisfy (
MI
) with matrix
P
where
\lambda_{P}=\frac{\vartheta}{2\alpha}
. Therefore, we can use Lemma
B.3
to derive the following inequality using the tower property of conditional expectations,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1})+\hat{k}_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}))}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}(\rho^{2}+\hat{k}_{\theta})\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\big(m_{k}^{\top}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}+\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|^{2}\big)}~\vline~\mathcal{F}_{k}\right]\right].

The risk-averseness parameter
\theta
satisfies (
57
), in particular
\theta<\frac{1}{4\alpha(\vartheta+\alpha L)}=\frac{1}{8\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})}
; therefore, we can see that
\frac{\theta\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})}{\sigma^{2}}<\frac{1}{8\sigma^{2}}
.

Applying inequality (
116
) with Cauchy-Schwarz and Hölder inequalities, the following conditional expectation can be bounded as,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\big(m_{k}^{\top}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}+\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|^{2}\big)}~\vline~\mathcal{F}_{k}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\big(\|m_{k}\|\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|+\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|^{2}\big)}~\vline~\mathcal{F}_{k}\right],

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{\sigma^{2}}\|m_{k}\|\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|}~\vline~\mathcal{F}_{k}]\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{\sigma^{2}}\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|^{2}}~\vline~\mathcal{F}_{k}]\right)^{1/2}.

Applying the inequalities (
115
) and (
116
) yields

\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\big(m_{k}^{\top}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}+\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}}\|^{2}\big)}~\vline~\mathcal{F}_{k}\right]\leq e^{\frac{2\theta^{2}\|m_{k}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}+2\theta\alpha^{2}(L+2\lambda_{P})}.

Recall
\|m_{k}\|^{2}\leq 8\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})+\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1}))
from Lemma
B.5
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1})+\hat{k}_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}))}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\big((\rho^{2}+\hat{k}_{\theta}+32\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}\theta)\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})+32\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}\theta\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1})\big)+2\theta\alpha^{2}(L+2\lambda_{P})}\right],

\displaystyle\leq e^{2\theta\alpha^{2}(L+2\lambda_{P})}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\hat{\rho}^{2}\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})+\hat{k}_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1})\big)}\right],

where the last inequality follows from (
118
) and

(\rho^{2}+\hat{k}_{\theta}+32\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}\theta)\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})+32\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}\theta\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1})\\
=(\rho^{2}+\hat{k}_{\theta}+32\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}\theta)\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})+\frac{32\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}\theta}{\rho^{2}+\hat{k}_{\theta}+32\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1})\big).

Next, we study the expectation on the right-hand side of the above inequality further. Again following similar arguments, it is straighforward to show:

\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\hat{\rho}^{2}\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})+\hat{k}_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1})\big)}\right]\\
\leq\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\hat{\rho}^{2}(\rho^{2}+\hat{k}_{\theta})\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1})}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta\hat{\rho}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\big(m_{k-1}^{\top}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}+\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}\|^{2}\big)}~\vline~\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\right].

Proceeding in a similar fashion,

\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta\hat{\rho}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\big(m_{k-1}^{\top}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}+\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}\|^{2}\big)}~\vline~\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta\hat{\rho}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\|m_{k-1}\|\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}\|}~\vline~\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta\hat{\rho}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}\|^{2}}~\vline~\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\right)^{1/2}.

Using the inequalities (
115
) and (
116
) together with Lemma
B.5
one more time for the conditional expectations on the right-hand side of the above inequality:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta\hat{\rho}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\big(m_{k-1}^{\top}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}+\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k}}\|^{2}\big)}~\vline~\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\leq e^{\frac{\theta^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}16\hat{\rho}^{4}\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1})+\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-2}))+2\theta\hat{\rho}^{2}\alpha^{2}(L+2\lambda_{P})}.

Since
\hat{\rho}^{2}<1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\hat{\rho}^{2}(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})+\hat{k}_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1}))}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\hat{\rho}^{2}\big((\rho^{2}+\hat{k}_{\theta}+32\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}\theta)\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1})+32\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}\theta\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-2})\big)+2\theta\hat{\rho}^{2}\alpha^{2}(L+2\lambda_{P})}\right],

\displaystyle\leq e^{2\theta\hat{\rho}^{2}\alpha^{2}(L+2\lambda_{P})}\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\hat{\rho}^{4}\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1})+\hat{k}_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-2})\big)}].

Notice that this inequality gives us

\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1})+\hat{k}_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})\big)}\right]\leq e^{2\theta\alpha^{2}(1+\hat{\rho}^{2})(L+2\lambda_{P})}\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\hat{\rho}^{4}\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1})+\hat{k}_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-2})\big)}],

and continuing this recursion for
k
-steps yields the inequality (
119
). Next, we provide a bound on the moment generating function of
\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{1})+\hat{k}_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})
. By Lemma
B.3
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta\hat{\rho}^{2k}}{2\sigma^{2}}(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{1})+\hat{k}_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0}))}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta\hat{\rho}^{2k}}{2\sigma^{2}}\big((\rho^{2}+\hat{k}_{\theta})\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})+m_{0}^{\top}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{1}}+\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{1}}\|^{2}\big)}].

Here,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0}
is the initialization of the algorithm and we take it to be deterministic. Also, using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality together with the Hölder inequality and the inequalities (
115
) and (
116
) yields,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta\hat{\rho}^{2k}}{2\sigma^{2}}(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{1})+\hat{k}_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0}))}\right]\leq e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\hat{\rho}^{2k}(\rho^{2}+\hat{k}_{\theta})\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})}\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta\hat{\rho}^{2k}}{2\sigma^{2}}\big(m_{0}^{\top}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{1}}+\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{1}}\|^{2}\big)}],

\displaystyle\leq e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\hat{\rho}^{2k}(\rho^{2}+\hat{k}_{\theta})\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})}e^{\frac{2\theta^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\hat{\rho}^{4k}\|m_{0}\|^{2}+2\theta\hat{\rho}^{2k}\alpha^{2}(L+2\lambda_{P})}.

Recall from the proof of Lemma
B.6
that
\|m_{0}\|^{2}\leq 16\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})
and
\hat{\rho}^{4k}<\hat{\rho}^{2k}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta\hat{\rho}^{2k}}{2\sigma^{2}}(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{1})+\hat{k}_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0}))}\right]

\displaystyle\leq e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\hat{\rho}^{2k}(\rho^{2}+\hat{k}_{\theta})\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})+\frac{\theta^{2}\hat{\rho}^{2k}}{\sigma^{2}}32\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})+2\theta\hat{\rho}^{2k}\alpha^{2}(L+2\lambda_{P})},

\displaystyle\leq e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\hat{\rho}^{2(k+1)}2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})+2\theta\hat{\rho}^{2k}\alpha^{2}(L+2\lambda_{P})}.

(120)

Combining (
120
) with (
119
) yields

\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1})+\hat{k}_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}))}]\leq e^{2\theta\alpha^{2}\big(L+2\lambda_{P}\big)\sum_{j=0}^{k}\hat{\rho}^{2j}+\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\hat{\rho}^{2(k+1)}2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})}.

Lastly, we can use the fact that the inequality

e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}(f(x_{k+1})-f(x_{*}))}\leq e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1})}<e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1})+\hat{k}_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}))},

holds pointwise and by taking expectations

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1})+\hat{k}_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}))}\right]\geq\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1})}\right]\geq\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}(f(x_{k+1})-f(x_{*}))}\right].

This gives the desired inequality (
117
). Our main result (
58
) then directly follows from (
117
) and the definition of the finite-horizon entropic risk:

\displaystyle\mathbf{r}_{k,\sigma^{2}}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta}\log\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}(f(x_{k})-f(x_{*}))}\right],

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}<\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta}\log\exp\left\{2\theta\alpha^{2}\big(L+2\lambda_{P}\big)\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\hat{\rho}^{2j}+\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\hat{\rho}^{2k}2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})\right\}.

This completes the proof.

C.3
Proof of Theorem
5

Throughout the proof we are going to drop the
\vartheta
and
\psi
in the notations
\alpha_{\vartheta,\psi},\beta_{\vartheta,\psi},\gamma_{\vartheta,\psi}
and
\rho_{\vartheta,\psi}
, and also drop
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
in
\hat{\mathtt{v}}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma},\hat{t}^{(\varphi)}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma},\hat{\rho}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}
, and
\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{(\varphi)}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}
for simplicity.
The proof of the theorem is similar to the proof of Theorem
3
and incorporates the bound (
58
) provided in Proposition
6
to derive an upper bound on EV@R. First notice that under the setting of Theorem
2
, if we set
\theta<\theta_{u}^{sg}
then the conditions of Proposition
6
are satisfied and the inequality (
58
) can be used as

\displaystyle EV@R_{1-\zeta}[f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})]

\displaystyle=\inf_{0<\theta}\{\mathbf{r}_{k,\sigma^{2}}(\theta)+\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta}\log(1/\zeta)\},

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq}\inf_{0<\theta<\theta_{u}^{sg}}\left\{\frac{4\sigma^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{2}(L+2\lambda_{P})}{1-\hat{\rho}^{2}}+\hat{\rho}^{2k}2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})+\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta}\log(1/\zeta)\right\},

(121)

where strict inequality
\theta<\theta_{u}^{sg}
is required for
\hat{\rho}^{2}<1
. Let us fix a constant
\varphi\in(0,1)
and set
\theta_{\varphi}^{sg}=\varphi\theta_{u}^{sg}
, then we can see that
\hat{\rho}^{2}
is increasing with respect to
\theta
and the following inequality holds for all
\theta<\theta_{\varphi}^{sg}
,

\displaystyle\hat{\rho}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\big(\rho^{2}+32\alpha^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\mathtt{v}}\theta\big)+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\rho^{2}+32\alpha^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\mathtt{v}}\theta)^{2}+128\alpha^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\mathtt{v}}\theta},

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{2}\big(\rho^{2}+32\alpha^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\mathtt{v}}\theta_{\varphi}^{sg}\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\rho^{2}+32\alpha^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\mathtt{v}}\theta_{\varphi}^{sg})^{2}+128\alpha^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\mathtt{v}}\theta_{\varphi}^{sg}}=\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{(\varphi)}.

Similarly, we can also write

\displaystyle\rho^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\big(\rho^{2}+32\alpha^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\mathtt{v}}\theta\big)+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\rho^{2}+32\alpha^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\mathtt{v}}\theta)^{2}+128\alpha^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\mathtt{v}}\theta},

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{2}\big(\rho^{2}+32\alpha^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\mathtt{v}}\theta\big)+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\rho^{2}+32\alpha^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\mathtt{v}}\theta_{\varphi}^{sg})^{2}+128\alpha^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\mathtt{v}}\theta_{\varphi}^{sg}}=\frac{1}{2}(\rho^{2}+32\alpha^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\mathtt{v}}\theta)+\frac{1}{2}\hat{t}^{(\varphi)},

on the region
(0,\theta_{\varphi}^{sg}]
. Therefore, the inequality (
C.3
) becomes

\displaystyle EV@R_{1-\zeta}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq}\inf_{0<\theta<\theta_{u}^{sg}}\left\{\frac{4\sigma^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{2}(L+2\lambda_{P})}{1-\hat{\rho}^{2}}+\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta}\log(1/\zeta)+\hat{\rho}^{2k}2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})\right\},

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq}\min_{0<\theta\leq\theta_{\varphi}^{sg}}\left\{\frac{8\sigma^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{2}(L+2\lambda_{P})}{2-\rho^{2}-\hat{t}^{(\varphi)}-32\alpha^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\mathtt{v}}\theta}+\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta}\log(1/\zeta)+(\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{(\varphi)})^{k}2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})\right\}.

It can be seen that the function

\hat{h}(\theta)=\frac{8\sigma^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{2}(L+2\lambda_{P})}{2-\rho^{2}-\hat{t}^{(\varphi)}-32\alpha^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\mathtt{v}}\theta}+\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta}\log(1/\zeta)+(\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{(\varphi)})^{k}2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0}),

is convex on the region
(0,\theta_{\varphi}^{sg}]
with a unique minimum. With a slight abuse of notation, let us introduce

\displaystyle\mathtt{c}_{0}=8\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{2}(L+2\lambda_{P}),\;\;\mathtt{c}_{1}=2-\rho^{2}-\hat{t}^{(\varphi)},\;\;\mathtt{c}_{2}=32\alpha^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\mathtt{v}}.

Since
\hat{\hat{\rho}}
and
\hat{t}^{(\varphi)}
do not depend on
\theta
, we can see that the derivative
\hat{h}^{\prime}(\theta)
can be written as

\hat{h}^{\prime}(\theta)=\frac{\sigma^{2}\mathtt{c}_{0}\mathtt{c}_{2}\theta^{2}-2\sigma^{2}(\mathtt{c}_{1}-\theta\mathtt{c}_{2})^{2}\log(1/\zeta)}{\theta^{2}(\mathtt{c}_{1}-\theta\mathtt{c}_{2})^{2}}.

The root,
\hat{\theta}_{*}
, of the polynomial at the nominator satisfying
\hat{\theta}_{*}\in(0,\frac{\mathtt{c}_{1}}{\mathtt{c}_{2}}]
is given as

\hat{\theta}_{*}=\frac{\mathtt{c}_{1}}{\mathtt{c}_{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\mathtt{c}_{0}\mathtt{c}_{2}}{2\log(1/\zeta)}}}.

We can see that

\displaystyle\mathtt{c}_{0}\mathtt{c}_{2}\theta^{2}-2(\mathtt{c}_{1}-\theta\mathtt{c}_{2})^{2}\log(1/\zeta)\leq 0

\displaystyle\;\;\text{ on }(0,\hat{\theta}_{*}],

\displaystyle\mathtt{c}_{0}\mathtt{c}_{2}\theta^{2}-2(\mathtt{c}_{1}-\theta\mathtt{c}_{2})^{2}\log(1/\zeta)>0

\displaystyle\;\;\text{ on }(\hat{\theta}_{*},\frac{\mathtt{c}_{1}}{\mathtt{c}_{2}}].

Therefore, we can conclude that the function
\hat{h}(\theta)
is decreasing on
(0,\hat{\theta}_{*}]
and increasing on
(\hat{\theta}_{*},\frac{\mathtt{c}_{1}}{\mathtt{c}_{2}}]
.
So far, we have shown that

EV@R_{1-\zeta}[f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})]\leq\min_{0<\theta\leq\theta_{\varphi}^{sg}}\hat{h}(\theta),

where
h
is convex on
(0,\theta_{\varphi}^{sg}]
and the derivative
\hat{h}^{\prime}
diminishes at
\hat{\theta}_{*}
. We obtain

EV@R_{1-\zeta}[f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})]{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq}\min_{0<\theta\leq\theta_{\varphi}^{sg}}\hat{h}(\theta)=\begin{cases}\hat{h}(\hat{\theta}_{*}),&\text{ if }\hat{\theta}_{*}\leq\theta_{\varphi}^{sg},\\
\hat{h}(\theta_{\varphi}^{sg}),&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(122)

By the definitions of
\mathtt{c}_{0},\mathtt{c}_{1}
, and
\mathtt{c}_{2}
, the condition (
60
) can be written as

\displaystyle\log(1/\zeta){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq}\frac{\mathtt{c}_{0}\mathtt{c}_{2}}{2}\Big(\frac{\theta_{\varphi}^{sg}}{\mathtt{c}_{1}-\theta_{\varphi}^{sg}\mathtt{c}_{2}}\Big)^{2}\implies\frac{\mathtt{c}_{1}-\theta_{\varphi}^{sg}\mathtt{c}_{2}}{\theta_{\varphi}^{sg}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq}\sqrt{\frac{\mathtt{c}_{0}\mathtt{c}_{2}}{2\log(1/\zeta)}}.

Hence,

\text{Condition \eqref{cond: conf-lev-subgauss}}\implies\hat{\theta}_{*}=\frac{\mathtt{c}_{1}}{\mathtt{c}_{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\mathtt{c}_{0}\mathtt{c}_{2}}{2\log(1/\zeta)}}}\leq\theta_{\varphi}^{sg}.

Moreover, we can calculate
\hat{h}(\theta_{*})
as

\displaystyle\hat{h}(\hat{\theta}_{*})

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma^{2}\mathtt{c}_{0}}{\mathtt{c}_{1}-\hat{\theta}_{*}\mathtt{c}_{2}}+\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\hat{\theta}_{*}}\log(1/\zeta)+(\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{(\varphi)})^{k}2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0}),

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\mathtt{c}_{1}}\sqrt{\mathtt{c}_{0}}\Big(\sqrt{2\mathtt{c}_{2}\log(1/\zeta)}+\sqrt{\mathtt{c}_{0}}\Big),

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\mathtt{c}_{1}}\sqrt{2\mathtt{c}_{2}\log(1/\zeta)}\Big(\sqrt{2\mathtt{c}_{2}\log(1/\zeta)}+\sqrt{\mathtt{c}_{0}}\Big)+(\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{(\varphi)})^{k}2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0}),

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\mathtt{c}_{1}}\Big(\sqrt{2\mathtt{c}_{2}\log(1/\zeta)}+\sqrt{\mathtt{c}_{0}}\Big)^{2}+(\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{(\varphi)})^{k}2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0}).

Therefore, if condition (
60
) holds then we have
\hat{\theta}_{*}<\theta_{\varphi}^{sg}
and the inequality (
122
) yields the inequality (
61
) from the calculation of
\hat{h}(\hat{\theta}_{*})
above. The inequality (
62
), also directly follows from (
122
) if
\theta_{\varphi}^{sg}>\hat{\theta}_{*}
. We can derive the inequality (
64
) by simply taking limit superior of both (
61
) and (
62
).

Appendix D
Proofs of Supplementary Results

D.1
Proof of Lemma
A.1

We denote the covariance matrix
\mathbb{E}[({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-\mathbb{E}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}])({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-\mathbb{E}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}])^{\top}]
by
\Xi_{k}
. Since the noise
w_{k}
is unbiased, we have
\mathbb{E}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1}]=A_{Q}\mathbb{E}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}]
. Therefore, we can write the following recursion,

\displaystyle\Xi_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1}-\mathbb{E}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1}])({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1}-\mathbb{E}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1}])^{\top}],

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(A_{Q}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-\mathbb{E}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}])+Bw_{k})(A_{Q}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-\mathbb{E}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}])+Bw_{k})^{\top}],

\displaystyle=A_{Q}\Xi_{k}A_{Q}^{\top}+\sigma^{2}BB^{\top}.

(123)

By the statement of Proposition
1
, we have
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in\mathcal{F}_{\theta}
, which also implies
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in\mathcal{S}_{q}
by Lemma
E.1
. Therefore, we have
\rho(A_{Q})<1
and the following discrete time Lyapunov equation holds for the limiting covariance matrix
\Xi_{\infty}=\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\Xi_{k}
:

\Xi_{\infty}=A_{Q}\Xi_{\infty}A_{Q}^{\top}+\sigma^{2}BB^{\top}.

(124)

Let
Q=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{Q}\Lambda\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{Q}^{\top}
be the eigendecomposition of the matrix
Q
, then we can write the following for
A_{Q}
,

\displaystyle A_{Q}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}(1+\beta)I_{d}-\alpha(1+\gamma)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{Q}\Lambda\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{Q}^{\top}&-(\beta-\alpha\gamma\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{Q}\Lambda\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{Q}^{\top})\\
I_{d}&0_{d}\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{Q}&0\\
0&\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{Q}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}(1+\beta)-\alpha(1+\gamma)\Lambda&-(\beta-\alpha\gamma\Lambda)\\
I_{d}&0_{d}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{Q}^{\top}&0_{d}\\
0_{d}&\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{Q}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}.

Define
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{\pi}
as the permutation matrix associated with permutation
\pi
over
\{1,2,...,2d\}
such that
\pi(i)=2i-1
for
i\in[1,d]
and
\pi(i)=2(i-d)
for
i\in[d+i,2d]
. It is well known that the permutation matrices are invertible and their inverses satisfy
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{\pi}^{-1}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{\pi}^{\top}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{\pi^{-1}}
. Hence, we can write
A_{Q}
as

A_{Q}=\underbrace{\begin{bmatrix}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{Q}&0_{d}\\
0_{d}&\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{Q}\end{bmatrix}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{\pi}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U}\tilde{A}_{Q}\underbrace{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{\pi}^{\top}\begin{bmatrix}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{Q}^{\top}&0_{d}\\
0_{d}&\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{Q}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U^{\top}},

where
\tilde{A}_{Q}:=\text{blkdiag}(\{M_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})
as given in (
67
)
Therefore, the equation (
124
) can be written as

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U^{\top}\Xi_{\infty}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U^{\top}A_{Q}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U^{\top}\Xi_{\infty}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\right)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U^{\top}A_{Q}^{\top}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U+\sigma^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U^{\top}BB^{\top}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U.

Introducing
\tilde{\Xi}_{\infty}=U^{\top}\Xi_{\infty}U
,
\tilde{A}_{Q}=U^{\top}A_{Q}U
, and
\tilde{B}=U^{\top}B
,
we get

\tilde{\Xi}_{\infty}=\tilde{A}_{Q}\tilde{\Xi}_{\infty}\tilde{A}_{Q}^{\top}+\sigma^{2}\tilde{B}\tilde{B}^{\top}.

It follows from this equation that the solution
\tilde{\Xi}_{\infty}=\underset{i=1,..,d}{\text{Diag}}\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{x}_{\lambda_{i}}&\tilde{y}_{\lambda_{i}}\\
\tilde{y}_{\lambda_{i}}&\tilde{v}_{\lambda_{i}}\end{bmatrix}
satisfies the following system of equations

\displaystyle\tilde{x}_{\lambda_{i}}

\displaystyle=c_{i}^{2}\tilde{x}_{\lambda_{i}}+2c_{i}d_{i}\tilde{y}_{\lambda_{i}}+d_{i}^{2}\tilde{v}_{\lambda_{i}}+\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2},

\displaystyle\tilde{y}_{\lambda_{i}}

\displaystyle=c_{i}\tilde{x}_{\lambda_{i}}+d_{i}\tilde{y}_{\lambda_{i}},

\displaystyle\tilde{v}_{\lambda_{i}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{x}_{\lambda_{i}},

where
c_{i}=1+\beta-\alpha(1+\gamma)\lambda_{i}
and
d_{i}=-\beta+\alpha\gamma\lambda_{i}
, and its solution is given as

\displaystyle\tilde{x}_{\lambda_{i}}=\frac{(1-d_{i})\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}}{(1+d_{i})[(1-d_{i})^{2}-c_{i}^{2}]},\;\;\tilde{y}_{\lambda_{i}}=\frac{c_{i}\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}}{(1+d_{i})[(1-d_{i})^{2}-c_{i}^{2}]},\;\;\tilde{v}_{\lambda_{i}}=\tilde{x}_{\lambda_{i}}.

Define
\Pi=[I_{d},0_{d}]\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times 2d}
with
d
-by-
d
zero matrix
0_{d}
. The matrix
Q^{1/2}\Sigma_{\infty}Q^{1/2}
is given by

Q^{1/2}\Sigma_{\infty}Q^{1/2}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q^{1/2}}\Pi\Xi_{\infty}\Pi^{\top}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q^{1/2}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q^{1/2}}\Pi\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\tilde{\Xi}_{\infty}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U^{\top}\Pi^{\top}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q^{1/2}}.

Therefore, its eigenvalues are

\lambda_{i}(Q^{1/2}\Sigma_{\infty}Q^{1/2})=\lambda_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q^{1/2}}\Pi\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\tilde{\Xi}_{\infty}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U^{\top}\Pi^{\top}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q^{1/2}})=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{\lambda_{i}(Q)(1-d_{i})\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}}{(1+d_{i})[(1-d_{i})^{2}-c_{i}^{2}]},

for
i=1,2,..,d
.

D.2
Proof of Lemma
A.2

We are first going to show that the covariance matrices of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{\infty}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}
satisfy
\Xi_{\infty}\succeq\Xi_{k}
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Since the initialization is deterministic, we have
\Xi_{0}=0
. Then the induction on
k
at the recursion (
123
) implies

\Xi_{k}=A_{Q}\Xi_{k-1}A_{Q}^{\top}+\sigma^{2}BB^{\top}=\sigma^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}(A_{Q}^{j})BB^{\top}(A_{Q}^{\top})^{j}.

By Lemma
E.1
, the assumption
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in\mathcal{F}_{\theta}
implies
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in\mathcal{S}_{q}
and
\rho(A_{Q})<1
. Therefore,
the solution
\Xi_{\infty}=\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\Xi_{k}
exists and has the form

\Xi_{\infty}=\sigma^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(A_{Q}^{j})BB^{\top}(A_{Q}^{\top})^{j}.

The matrix
A_{Q}^{j}BB^{\top}(A_{Q}^{\top})^{j}
is a positive semi-definite matrix for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
,

\Xi_{\infty}=\sigma^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(A_{Q}^{j})BB^{\top}(A_{Q}^{\top})^{j}\succeq\sigma^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}(A_{Q}^{j})BB^{\top}(A_{Q}^{\top})^{j}=\Xi_{k},

for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Therefore, we can conclude that
\Xi_{\infty}-\Xi_{k}\succeq 0
. Notice that the first
d
-by-
d
leading principle submatrix of
\Xi_{\infty}-\Xi_{k}
is
\Sigma_{\infty}-\Sigma_{k}
by definition; therefore,
\Xi_{\infty}-\Xi_{k}
is positive semidefinite if
\Sigma_{\infty}-\Sigma_{k}
and its Schur complement in
\Xi_{\infty}-\Xi_{k}
are also positive semidefinite. This shows that
\Sigma_{\infty}\succeq\Sigma_{k}
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Next, we will show
X=I-\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\Sigma_{k}^{1/2}Q\Sigma_{k}^{1/2}
and
Y=I-\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\Sigma_{\infty}^{1/2}Q\Sigma_{\infty}^{1/2}
are positive definite. Notice that
X\succeq Y
since
\Sigma_{\infty}\succeq\Sigma_{k}
; so, it is sufficient to show that
Y\succ 0
to complete the proof. The result directly follows from the fact that the eigenvalues of
\Sigma_{\infty}^{1/2}Q\Sigma_{\infty}^{1/2}
and
Q^{1/2}\Sigma_{\infty}Q^{1/2}
are equal and given as
\frac{\sigma^{2}}{u_{i}}
(from Lemma
A.1
). Therefore, for each
i=1,..,d

\lambda_{i}(\Sigma_{\infty}^{1/2}Q\Sigma^{1/2}_{\infty})\prec\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\theta}I_{d}
on
\mathcal{F}_{\theta}
which implies that
Y\succ 0
. This gives the desired result.

D.3
Proof of Lemma
B.3

Recall that
y_{k}=x_{k}+\gamma(x_{k}-x_{k-1})
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}=\tilde{\nabla}f(y_{k})-\nabla f(y_{k})
. Then the
L
-smoothness of
f
implies

\displaystyle f(y_{k})-f(x_{k+1})

\displaystyle\geq\nabla f(y_{k})^{\top}\big(\delta(x_{k-1}-x_{k})+\alpha(\nabla f(y_{k})+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad-\frac{L}{2}\|\delta(x_{k-1}-x_{k})+\alpha(\nabla f(y_{k})+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1})\|^{2},

\displaystyle=\nabla f(y_{k})^{\top}\big(\delta(x_{k-1}-x_{k})+\alpha\nabla f(y_{k})\big)-\frac{L}{2}\|\delta(x_{k-1}-x_{k})+\alpha\nabla f(y_{k})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\alpha(1-\alpha L)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}^{\top}\nabla f(y_{k})-\alpha\delta L\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}^{\top}(x_{k-1}-x_{k})-\frac{L\alpha^{2}}{2}\|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}\|^{2},

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix}^{\top}(\tilde{X}_{1}\otimes I_{d})\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix}^{\top}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}^{\top}n_{k}-\frac{L\alpha^{2}}{2}\|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}\|^{2},

where
\delta=\beta-\gamma
and
n_{k}=\alpha\delta L(x_{k-1}-x_{k})-\alpha(1-\alpha L)\nabla f(y_{k})
. Similarly, we can use the
\mu
-strong convexity to obtain the inequalities

\displaystyle f(x_{k})-f(y_{k})

\displaystyle\geq\nabla f(y_{k})^{\top}(x_{k}-y_{k})+\frac{\mu}{2}\|x_{k}-y_{k}\|^{2},

\displaystyle=\gamma\nabla f(y_{k})^{\top}\big(x_{k-1}-x_{k}\big)+\frac{\mu\gamma^{2}}{2}\|x_{k-1}-x_{k}\|^{2},

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix}^{\top}(\tilde{X}_{2}\otimes I_{d})\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix},

and

\displaystyle f(x_{*})-f(y_{k})

\displaystyle\geq\nabla f(y_{k})^{\top}(x_{*}-y_{k})+\frac{\mu}{2}\|x_{*}-y_{k}\|^{2},

\displaystyle=-\nabla f(y_{k})^{\top}\big((1+\gamma)(x_{k}-x_{*})-\gamma(x_{k-1}-x_{*})\big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\frac{\mu}{2}\|(1+\gamma)(x_{k}-x_{*})-\eta(x_{k-1}-x_{*})\|^{2},

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix}^{\top}(\tilde{X}_{3}\otimes I_{d})\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix}.

Thus we get

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix}^{\top}\big((\tilde{X}_{1}+\tilde{X}_{2})\otimes I_{d}\big)\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\leq f(x_{k})-f(x_{k+1})+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}^{\top}n_{k}+\frac{L\alpha^{2}}{2}\|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}\|^{2},

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix}^{\top}\big((\tilde{X}_{1}+\tilde{X}_{3})\otimes I_{d}\big)\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\leq f(x_{*})-f(x_{k+1})+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}^{\top}n_{k}+\frac{L\alpha^{2}}{2}\|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}\|^{2}.

Notice that above inequality gives us

f(x_{k+1})-f(x_{*})\leq\rho^{2}\left(f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})\right)-\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix}^{\top}(\tilde{X}\otimes I_{d})\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix}+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}^{\top}n_{k}+\frac{L\alpha^{2}}{2}\|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}\|^{2}.

(125)

We notice that

\displaystyle({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1}-z_{*})^{\top}P({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1}-z_{*})

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}A^{\top}PA&A^{\top}PB\\
B^{\top}PA&B^{\top}PB\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\;=\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix}^{\top}\begin{bmatrix}A^{\top}PA&A^{\top}PB\\
B^{\top}PA&B^{\top}PB\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+2\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}^{\top}B^{\top}P(A({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*})+B\nabla f(y_{k}))+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}^{\top}B^{\top}PB\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}.

Let us define
n^{\prime}_{k}=B^{\top}P(A({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*})+B\nabla f(y_{k}))
. Since the inequality (
MI
) holds:

({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1}^{\top}-z_{*})P({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1}-z_{*})\\
\leq\rho^{2}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*})^{\top}P({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*})+\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix}(\tilde{X}\otimes I_{d})\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix}+2\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}^{\top}n^{\prime}_{k}+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}^{\top}B^{\top}PB\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}.

(126)

Finally, recall that
B=[-\alpha I_{d},0_{d}]^{\top}
; therefore, if we combine inequalities (
125
) and (
126
), and the definition of the matrix
B
, we obtain

f(x_{k+1})-f(x_{*})+({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*})^{\top}P({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*})\leq\rho^{2}(f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})+({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*})^{\top}P({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}))\\
+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}^{\top}(n_{k}+2n^{\prime}_{k})+\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{k+1}\|^{2},

(127)

where
\lambda_{P}
is the largest eigenvalue of first
d
-by-
d
principle submatrix of
P
. This completes the proof.

D.4
Proof of Lemma
B.4

We introduce
\tilde{\theta}:=\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}
. If
\tilde{\theta}<\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}\alpha^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(2\lambda_{P}+L)}}
holds, we can write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\tilde{\theta}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}m}^{\top}w+\alpha^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\lambda_{P}+L/2)}\|w\|^{2})}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d/2}\det(\Sigma_{w})^{1/2}}\int e^{\tilde{\theta}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}m}^{\top}w+\alpha^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\lambda_{P}+L/2)}\|w\|^{2})-\frac{1}{2}w^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}_{w}w}dw,

\displaystyle\quad\quad=\frac{e^{\frac{\tilde{\theta}^{2}}{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}m}^{\top}(\Sigma^{-1}_{w}-\tilde{\theta}\alpha^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(2\lambda_{P}+L)}I_{d})^{-1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}m}}}{(2\pi)^{d/2}\det(\Sigma_{w})^{1/2}}\int e^{-\frac{1}{2}(w-w_{*})^{\top}(\Sigma^{-1}_{w}-\tilde{\theta}\alpha^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(2\lambda_{P}+L)}I_{d})(w-w_{*})}dw,

where
w_{*}=\tilde{\theta}(\Sigma^{-1}_{w}-\tilde{\theta}\alpha^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(2\lambda_{P}+L)}I_{d})^{-1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}m}
and
\Sigma_{w}=\sigma^{2}I_{d}
. Notice that
\Sigma^{-1}=\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}I_{d}
hence the inverse exists from the fact that
\tilde{\theta}<\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}\alpha^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(2\lambda_{P}+L)}}
. The right hand-side is a Gaussian integral and yields:

\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\tilde{\theta}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}m}^{\top}w^{g}+\alpha^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\lambda_{P}+L/2)}\|w^{g}\|^{2})}\right]=\frac{1}{\det(I-\tilde{\theta}\alpha^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(2\lambda_{P}+L)}\Sigma_{w})^{1/2}}e^{\frac{\tilde{\theta}^{2}}{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}m}^{\top}(\Sigma^{-1}_{w}-\tilde{\theta}\alpha^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(2\lambda_{P}+L)}I_{d})^{-1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}m}}.

(128)

Using
\Sigma_{w}=\sigma^{2}I_{d}
and writing
\tilde{\theta}=\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}
back yields the desired result.

D.5
Proof of Lemma
B.5

We are first going to bound the norms of vectors
n_{k}=\alpha\delta L(x_{k-1}-x_{k})-\alpha(1-\alpha L)\nabla f(y_{k})
and
n^{\prime}_{k}:=B^{\top}P(A({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*})+B\nabla f(y_{k}))
given in the proof of Lemma
B.3
. Notice that
m_{k}=n_{k}+2n^{\prime}_{k}
; so, these bounds directly provides an upper bound on
\|m_{k}\|^{2}
. We will first prove

\|n_{k}\|^{2}\leq\frac{4\alpha^{2}L^{2}}{\mu}\left(2\delta^{2}+(1-\alpha L)^{2}(1+2\gamma(1+\gamma))\right)\left(\mathcal{V}_{P}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})+\mathcal{V}_{P}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1})\right).

(129)

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\|n_{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\alpha\delta L(x_{k-1}-x_{k})-\alpha(1-\alpha L)\nabla f(y_{k})\|^{2},

\displaystyle\leq 2(\alpha L\delta)^{2}\|x_{k-1}-x_{k}\|^{2}+2\alpha^{2}(1-\alpha L)^{2}\|\nabla f(y_{k})\|^{2}.

(130)

Recall that
\nabla f(x_{*})=0
and
f
is
L
-smooth:

\displaystyle\|\nabla f(y_{k})\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\nabla f(y_{k})-\nabla f(x_{*})\|^{2}\leq L^{2}\|y_{k}-x_{*}\|^{2},

\displaystyle=L^{2}\|(1+\gamma)(x_{k}-x_{*})-\gamma(x_{k-1}-x_{*})\|^{2},

\displaystyle=L^{2}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*})^{\top}\left(\begin{bmatrix}(1+\gamma)^{2}&-\gamma(1+\gamma)\\
-\gamma(1+\gamma)&\gamma^{2}\end{bmatrix}\otimes I_{d}\right)({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}).

The eigenvalues of the matrix on the right-hand side of this equality are
0
and
(1+\gamma)^{2}+\gamma^{2}
which implies,

\|\nabla f(y_{k})\|^{2}\leq L^{2}(1+2\gamma(1+\gamma))\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\|^{2}.

(131)

On the other hand, we have the inequality
\|x_{k-1}-x_{k}\|^{2}\leq 2\|x_{k}-x_{*}\|^{2}+2\|x_{k-1}-x_{*}\|^{2}=2\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\|^{2}
. Combining the bounds (
D.5
) and (
131
) yields,

\|n_{k}\|^{2}\leq 2\alpha^{2}L^{2}\left(2\delta^{2}+(1-\alpha L)^{2}(1+2\gamma(1+\gamma))\right)\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\|^{2}.

(132)

The
\mu
-strong convexity and the the positive semi-definiteness of
P
imply

\displaystyle\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\|x_{k}-x_{*}\|^{2}+\|x_{k-1}-x_{*}\|^{2}\leq\frac{2}{\mu}\big(f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})\big)+\frac{2}{\mu}\big(f(x_{k-1})-f(x_{*})\big),

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\mu}\left(\mathcal{V}_{P}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})+\mathcal{V}_{P}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1})\right),

which together with (
132
) shows (
129
).
Next, we show
n^{\prime}_{k}
admits the bound

\|n^{\prime}_{k}\|^{2}\leq\alpha^{2}\lambda_{p}\rho^{2}\mathcal{V}_{P}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}).

(133)

Remember that
P
is positive semidefinite matrix; therefore, there exists a square root matrix
P^{1/2}
such that
P^{1/2}P^{1/2}=P
. Hence,

\displaystyle\|n^{\prime}_{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\|(B^{\top}P^{1/2})(P^{1/2}[A({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*})+B\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\nabla f(y_{k})])\|^{2},

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{2}\lambda_{P}\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix}^{\top}\begin{bmatrix}A^{\top}PA&A^{\top}PB\\
B^{\top}PA&B^{\top}PB\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix}.

The matrix inequality (
MI
) implies

\displaystyle\|n^{\prime}_{k}\|^{2}\leq\alpha^{2}\lambda_{P}\left[\rho^{2}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*})^{\top}P({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*})+\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix}^{\top}(\tilde{X}\otimes I_{d})\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix}\right].

Because the function
f
is
L
-smooth and
\mu
-strongly convex, the matrix
\tilde{X}\otimes I_{d}
satisfies the following inequality (see
[
20
, Lemma 5]
)

\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix}(\tilde{X}\otimes I_{d})\begin{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*}\\
\nabla f(y_{k})\end{bmatrix}\leq\rho^{2}(f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})),

which yields the desired result:

\|n^{\prime}_{k}\|^{2}\leq\alpha^{2}\lambda_{P}\rho^{2}\left[({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*})^{\top}P({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{*})+f(x_{k})-f(x_{*})\right]=\alpha^{2}\lambda_{P}\rho^{2}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}).

By applying Cauchy-Schwarz again,

\|m_{k}\|^{2}=\|n_{k}+2n^{\prime}_{k}\|^{2}\leq 2\|n_{k}\|^{2}+8\|n^{\prime}_{k}\|^{2}.

Therefore, combining everything

\|m_{k}\|^{2}\leq 8\alpha^{2}\left(\frac{L^{2}\mathtt{v}^{\prime}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}}{\mu}+\lambda_{P}\rho^{2}\right)\mathcal{V}_{P}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})+\frac{8\alpha^{2}L^{2}\mathtt{v}^{\prime}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}}{\mu}\mathcal{V}_{P}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1}),

where
\mathtt{v}^{\prime}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}=2\delta^{2}+(1-\alpha L)^{2}(1+2\gamma(1+\gamma))
. Notice that
\mathtt{v}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}=\left(\frac{2L^{2}\mathtt{v}^{\prime}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}}{\mu}+\lambda_{P}\rho^{2}\right)
, which completes the proof.

D.6
Proof of Lemma
B.6

Let us drop the
\vartheta,\psi
dependency on the notations
\alpha_{\vartheta,\psi},\beta_{\vartheta,\psi},\gamma_{\vartheta,\psi},\mathtt{v}_{\vartheta,\psi}
,
\bar{\rho}_{\vartheta,\psi}
, and
\rho_{\vartheta,\psi}
, and use
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\mathtt{v},\bar{\rho}
, and
\rho
instead for simplicity. Also let us denote
\mathcal{V}_{P}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z})
by
\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z})
. Recall that
\lambda_{P}
is the largest eigenvalue of the
d
-by-
d
principle submatrix
P
and is given as
\lambda_{P}=\frac{\vartheta}{2\alpha}
by Theorem
2
. Define the variables

\displaystyle l_{\theta}=\frac{\theta 4\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}}{2-\theta\alpha^{2}(2\lambda_{P}+L)},\text{ and }k_{\theta}=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\rho^{2}+l_{\theta})^{2}+4l_{\theta}}-\frac{(\rho^{2}+l_{\theta})}{2}.

Notice that
\bar{\rho}^{2}=\rho^{2}+k_{\theta}+l_{\theta}
by definition (
39
). Before starting the proof, we point out some properties of
k_{\theta}
and
\bar{\rho}
which will be useful to obtain our results. First of all by definition of
\rho^{2}
and
\lambda_{P}
given in Theorem
2
, the variable
\theta_{u}^{g}
can be written as

\theta_{u}^{g}=\frac{2(1-\rho^{2})}{8\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}+\alpha^{2}(1-\rho^{2})(2\lambda_{P}+L)},

therefore, the condition (
37
) yields

\displaystyle\theta<\theta_{u}^{g}

\displaystyle\implies\theta 8\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}+\theta\alpha^{2}(1-\rho^{2})(2\lambda_{P}+L)<2(1-\rho^{2}),

\displaystyle\implies\theta 8\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}<2(1-\rho^{2})-\theta\alpha^{2}(1-\rho^{2})(2\lambda_{P}+L),

\displaystyle\implies\frac{\theta 4\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}}{2-\theta\alpha^{2}(2\lambda_{P}+L)}<\frac{1-\rho^{2}}{2}.

Hence, this implies

\displaystyle 1-\rho^{2}>2l_{\theta}

\displaystyle\implies 4(1-\rho^{2})-4l_{\theta}>4l_{\theta},

\displaystyle\implies 4-4(\rho^{2}+l_{\theta})+(\rho^{2}+l_{\theta})^{2}>(\rho^{2}+l_{\theta})^{2}+4l_{\theta},

\displaystyle\implies(2-(\rho^{2}+l_{\theta}))^{2}>(\rho^{2}+l_{\theta})^{2}+4l_{\theta},

\displaystyle\implies 1-\rho^{2}-l_{\theta}>\frac{1}{2}\left(\sqrt{(\rho^{2}+l_{\theta})^{2}+4l_{\theta}}-(\rho^{2}+l_{\theta})\right),

\displaystyle\implies 1>\rho^{2}+k_{\theta}+l_{\theta}=\bar{\rho}^{2}.

(134)

Hence, we can see that
\bar{\rho}<1
. Secondly, the definition of
k_{\theta}
shows us the following relation

(k_{\theta})^{2}+k_{\theta}(\rho^{2}+l_{\theta})-l_{\theta}=0\implies k_{\theta}=\frac{l_{\theta}}{\rho^{2}+k_{\theta}+l_{\theta}}.

(135)

After presenting these properties of
\bar{\rho}
and
k_{\theta}
, we now focus on our proof. In order to show (
106
), we are first going to prove the following inequality,

\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1})+k_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})\big)}]\leq\prod_{j=0}^{k-1}\big(1-\theta\bar{\rho}^{2j}\alpha^{2}(\lambda_{P}+\frac{L}{2})\big)^{-d/2}\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\bar{\rho}^{2k}(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{1})+k_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0}))}],

(136)

where
\theta<\frac{2}{\alpha^{2}(2\lambda_{P}+L)}
. Let us show that (
136
) holds for
k=1
to have an insight. Notice that if
\theta
satisfies condition (
37
) then it also satisfies
\theta<\frac{2}{\alpha^{2}(2\lambda_{P}+L)}
(see the (
101
)) and by the statement of Lemma
B.6
the conditions of Lemma
B.3
also hold. Therefore, we can write the following using Lemmata
B.3
-
B.4
-
B.5
and the fact that the noise
w_{1}
is independent from
\mathcal{F}_{1}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{2})+k_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{1})\big)}]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\big((\rho^{2}+k_{\theta})\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{1})+m_{1}^{\top}w_{1}+\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\|w_{1}\|^{2}\big)}\right],

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}(\rho^{2}+k_{\theta})\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{1})}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\big(m_{1}^{\top}w_{1}+\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\|w_{1}\|^{2}\big)}\mid\mathcal{F}_{1}\right]\right],

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\big(1-\theta\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\big)^{-d/2}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\big((\rho^{2}+k_{\theta})\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{1})+\frac{\theta 4\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}}{2-\theta\alpha^{2}(2\lambda_{P}+L)}(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{1})+\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0}))}\right],

\displaystyle\qquad=\big(1-\theta\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\big)^{-d/2}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}(\rho^{2}+k_{\theta}+l_{\theta})\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{1})+\frac{l_{\theta}}{\rho^{2}+k_{\theta}+l_{\theta}}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})\big)}\right].

We obtain the inequality (
136
) for
k=1
using the equality (
135
) and the definition of
\bar{\rho}^{2}
:

\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{2})+k_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{1})\big)}]\leq\Big(1-\theta\alpha^{2}\big(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P}\big)\Big)^{-d/2}\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\bar{\rho}^{2}\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{1})+k_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})\big)}].

We have already shown that
\theta<\frac{2}{\alpha^{2}(2\lambda_{P}+L)}
and assumed the conditions of Lemma
B.3
; so, we can use Lemmata
B.3
-
B.4
-
B.5
and we can write the following inequality for
k+1
by following similar approach,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1})+k_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})\big)}]\leq\big(1-\theta\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\big)^{-d/2}\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\bar{\rho}^{2}\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})+k_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1})\big)}].

Moreover, since
\theta\bar{\rho}^{2}<\theta<\frac{2}{\alpha^{2}(2\lambda_{P}+L)}
, we can also use Lemmas
B.3
-
B.4
-
B.5
on the integral on the right-hand side of the inequality above:

\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\bar{\rho}^{2}(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})+k_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1}))}]\leq(1-\theta\bar{\rho}^{2}\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P}))^{-d/2}\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\bar{\rho}^{2}\big((\rho^{2}+k_{\theta})\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1})+\frac{\theta\bar{\rho}^{2}4\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}}{2-\theta\bar{\rho}^{2}\alpha^{2}(2\lambda_{P}+L)}(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1})+\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-2}))\big)}].

(137)

Notice that
l_{\theta}
is increasing with respect to
\theta>0
, that is,

l_{\theta\bar{\rho}^{2}}=\frac{\theta\bar{\rho}^{2}4\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}}{2-\theta\bar{\rho}^{2}\alpha^{2}(2\lambda_{P}+L)}<\frac{\theta 4\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}}{2-\theta\alpha^{2}(2\lambda_{P}+L)}=l_{\theta}.

Since
\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1})
and
\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-2})
are all non-negative, we can convert (
137
) into following using our observation on
l^{\theta}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\bar{\rho}^{2}\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})+k_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1})\big)}]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\leq(1-\theta\bar{\rho}^{2}\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P}))^{-d/2}\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\bar{\rho}^{2}\big((\rho^{2}+k_{\theta}+l_{\theta})\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1})+l_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-2})\big)}],

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\leq\big(1-\theta\bar{\rho}^{2}\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\big)^{-d/2}\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}(\bar{\rho}^{2})^{2}\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1})+k_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-2})\big)}].

So far we obtain,

\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1})+k_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})\big)}]\leq\big(1-\theta\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\big)^{-d/2}\big(1-\theta\bar{\rho}^{2}\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\big)^{-d/2}\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\bar{\rho}^{4}\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1})+k_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-2})\big)}\Big],

and repeating these steps for
k
-times gives the desired inequality (
136
).

Next, we are going to study
\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\bar{\rho}^{2k}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{1})}]
. Again, Lemmata
B.3
and
B.4
yield

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\bar{\rho}^{2k}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{1})}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta\bar{\rho}^{2k}}{2\sigma^{2}}\big(\rho^{2}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})+m_{0}^{\top}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{1}}+\alpha^{2}(\frac{L}{2}+\lambda_{P})\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{1}}\|^{2}\big)}\right],

\displaystyle\leq\big(1-\theta\bar{\rho}^{2k}\alpha^{2}(\lambda_{P}+\frac{L}{2})\big)^{-d/2}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta\bar{\rho}^{2k}}{2\sigma^{2}}\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})+\frac{\theta\bar{\rho}^{2k}\|m_{0}\|^{2}}{2(2-\theta\bar{\rho}^{2k}\alpha^{2}(2\lambda_{P}+L))}\big)}\right].

On the other hand, we can see from the proof of Lemma
B.5
that
\|m_{0}\|^{2}<16\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})
and

\frac{\theta\bar{\rho}^{2k}}{2-\theta\bar{\rho}^{2k}\alpha^{2}(2\lambda_{P}+L)}\leq\frac{\theta}{2-\theta\alpha^{2}(2\lambda_{P}+L)}.

Hence above inequality becomes

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\bar{\rho}^{2k}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{1})}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\big(1-\theta\bar{\rho}^{2k}\alpha^{2}(\lambda_{P}+\frac{L}{2})\big)^{-d/2}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\theta\bar{\rho}^{2k}}{2\sigma^{2}}\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})+\frac{\theta 8\alpha^{2}\mathtt{v}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})}{(2-\theta\alpha^{2}(2\lambda_{P}+L))}\big)}\right],

\displaystyle\leq\big(1-\theta\bar{\rho}^{2k}\alpha^{2}(\lambda_{P}+\frac{L}{2})\big)^{-d/2}e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}(\rho^{2}+l_{\theta})2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})}.

(138)

Combining the inequality (
136
) with (
138
) yields

\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\big(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k+1})+k_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})\big)}]\leq\prod_{j=0}^{k}\big(1-\theta\bar{\rho}^{2j}\alpha^{2}(\lambda_{P}+\frac{L}{2})\big)^{-d/2}e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\bar{\rho}^{2(k+1)}2\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{0})},

(139)

Let
k\geq 1
. Notice that
\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})\geq 0
for any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}
and
\theta,k_{\theta}>0
; hence, the inequality
e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})+k_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1}))}>e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})}
holds pointwise. Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}(\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})+k_{\theta}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k-1}))}]\geq\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}\mathcal{V}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}_{k})}]\geq\mathbb{E}[e^{\frac{\theta}{2\sigma^{2}}(f(x_{k})-f(x_{*}))}].

This gives the desired result.

Appendix E
Relationship between the sets
\mathcal{F}_{\theta}
and
S_{q}

Lemma E.1
.

The
\theta
-feasible set satisfies
\mathcal{F}_{\theta}\subset\mathcal{S}_{q}
; moreover, we have
\lim_{\theta\rightarrow 0}\mathcal{F}_{\theta}=\mathcal{S}_{q}=\{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)~|~|c_{i}|\leq|1-d_{i}|\;\text{and}\;0<u_{i}\}
.

Proof.

We are first going to show
\mathcal{F}_{\theta}\subset\mathcal{S}_{q}
.
Let
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in\mathcal{F}_{\theta}
for a given
\theta>0
, then
|c_{i}|<|1-d_{i}|
and
u_{i}>\frac{\theta}{2}
for all
u_{i}
. Since
\theta>0
, this also implies
u_{i}>0
and the inequality
|c_{i}|<|1-d_{i}|
gives
(1-d_{i})^{2}-c_{i}^{2}>0
. Recall that
Q
is positive definite; hence, we can write

u_{i}=\frac{(1+d_{i})[(1-d_{i})^{2}-c_{i}^{2}]}{\lambda_{i}(Q)(1-d_{i})\alpha^{2}}>\theta>0\implies\frac{1+d_{i}}{1-d_{i}}>0\implies 1>|d_{i}|.

(140)

The stable is defined as
\mathcal{S}_{q}:=\{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in\mathbb{R}_{++}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}~|~\rho(A_{Q})=\max\{\rho_{i}\}<1\}
, where
\rho_{i}
is given as

\rho_{i}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}|c_{i}|+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{c_{i}^{2}+4d_{i}},&\text{ if }c_{i}^{2}+4d_{i}>0,\\
\sqrt{|d_{i}|},&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

The inequality (
140
) already shows that
\rho_{i}<1
for
c_{i}^{2}+4d_{i}<0
. Next, we will show
|c_{i}|+\sqrt{c_{i}^{2}+4d_{i}}<2
for
c_{i}^{2}+4d_{i}
, which in turn shows
\rho_{i}<1
. Suppose
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in\mathcal{F}_{\theta}
and
c_{i}^{2}+4d_{i}>0
. Since, we have already shown
|d_{i}|<1
, we can use
1-d_{i}>|c_{i}|
and write

\displaystyle 1-d_{i}>|c_{i}|

\displaystyle\implies 4(1-d_{i})>4|c_{i}|\implies 4-4|c_{i}|+c_{i}^{2}>c_{i}^{2}+4d_{i},

\displaystyle\implies(2-|c_{i}|)^{2}>c_{i}^{2}+4d_{i}\implies 2>|c_{i}|+\sqrt{c_{i}^{2}+4d_{i}}.

(141)

This shows that
\rho_{i}<1
in the case
c_{i}^{2}+4d_{i}>0
for each
i=1,...,d
.Therefore, (
140
) and (
141
) show us that for all
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in\mathcal{F}_{\theta}
then
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in\mathcal{S}_{q}
proving
\mathcal{F}_{\theta}\subset\mathcal{S}_{q}
. Notice that these inequalities are derived by using the property
\theta>0
. Matter of fact, we can show all of the results only using
u_{i}>0
. This also yields
\underset{\theta>0}{\cup}\mathcal{F}_{\theta}\subset\mathcal{S}_{q}
. Lastly, let us show
\mathcal{S}_{q}\subset\underset{\theta>0}{\cup}\mathcal{F}_{\theta}
, so that we can prove
\underset{\theta>0}{\cup}\mathcal{F}_{\theta}=\mathcal{S}_{q}
. In order to show this, we are going to use properties of Lyapunov stability. Particularly, since
\rho(A_{Q})<1
on
\mathcal{S}_{q}
the following Lyapunov equation (
89
) holds

\tilde{V}_{\infty}=\tilde{A}_{\Lambda}\tilde{V}_{\infty}\tilde{A}_{\Lambda}^{\top}+\sigma^{2}\tilde{B}\tilde{B}^{\top},

(142)

for a matrix
\tilde{V}_{\infty}=\underset{i=1,..,d}{\mbox{Diag}}\begin{bmatrix}\bar{x}_{i}&\bar{y}_{i}\\
\bar{y}_{i}&\bar{v}_{i}\end{bmatrix}
with entries

\displaystyle\bar{x}_{\lambda_{i}}=\frac{(1-d_{i})\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}}{(1+d_{i})[(1-d_{i})^{2}-c_{i}^{2}]},\;\;\bar{y}_{\lambda_{i}}=\frac{c_{i}\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}}{(1+d_{i})[(1-d_{i})^{2}-c_{i}^{2}]},\;\;\bar{v}_{\lambda_{i}}=\bar{x}_{\lambda_{i}},

Since the solution exists, it follows from Lyapunov theory that the matrix
\tilde{V}_{\infty}
is positive semidefinite. This holds if and only if

\bar{x}_{\lambda_{i}}=\frac{(1-d_{i})\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}}{(1+d_{i})[(1-d_{i})^{2}-c_{i}^{2}]}>0,

and the determinant is non-negative

\bar{x}_{\lambda_{i}}^{2}-\bar{y}_{\lambda_{i}}^{2}=\frac{[(1-d_{i})^{2}-c_{i}^{2}]\alpha^{4}\sigma^{4}}{(1+d_{i})^{2}[(1-d_{i})^{2}-c_{i}^{2}]^{2}}>0.

for all
i=1,..,d
.These yield the conditions

u_{i}>0\text{ and }(1-d_{i})^{2}>c_{i}^{2},

which in turn implies
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in\mathcal{F}_{\theta}
. This gives the desired result
\lim_{\theta\rightarrow 0}\mathcal{F}_{\theta}=\mathcal{S}_{q}
, where we used the fact that the sets
\mathcal{F}_{\theta}
are monotonically increasing as
\theta
decreases, i.e.
\mathcal{F}_{\theta_{1}}\subset\mathcal{F}_{\theta_{2}}
for the
\theta_{1}>\theta_{2}
. Finally, we can compute
\lim_{\theta\rightarrow 0}\mathcal{F}_{\theta}
by inserting
\theta=0
in (
20
).
∎
